A\r\n  gate written to catch an install failure that only ever linked cannot see the defect it exists for.\r\n- **Write the swap to a script and run the file**, so the build, the pack, and the install are one\r\n  artifact the next run reuses rather than a command nobody can read back.\r\n- **Record the range you replaced** in the same step that replaces it. A consumer sitting on an\r\n  unpublished tarball with no record of what it had is a consumer nobody can restore.\r\n- **Rebuild and repack whenever the source moves.** A stale tarball is the same defect as a stale\r\n  `dist/`, and it is worse for being invisible: the consumer's gates go green against a fix that no\r\n  longer exists in the dependency's tree.\r\n- **Run one unit per checkout, at that checkout's catalog layer.** Give a checkout with no rows an\r\n  adopt unit only when its typecheck against the staged closure reddens.\r\n- **Fetch and merge the dependency's default branch before packing it**, wherever another session\r\n  can move that branch. A pack from a stale tip ships the consumer a dependency the dependency's own\r\n  repository no longer has.\r\n- **Restore the registry copy before any gate that must prove the published artifact, and before\r\n  publishing anything.** A distribution proof run against a local tarball proves the local tarball.\r\n  The release still follows layer order: the dependency publishes first, then the consumer re-pins to\r\n  the version the registry now serves and re-runs its gates against that.\r\n- **Keep the tarballs out of the tree.** They belong under `tmp/`, they are swept at acceptance, and\r\n  they are never committed.\r\n\r\nThe tarball is a head start, not a shortcut. It lets the consumer's work proceed and its proofs run\r\nagainst the real packed artifact while the dependency's own release is still being prepared.\r\n\r\n### What a bump obliges\r\n\r\nA runtime dependency and a development dependency have different blast radius, and confusing them\r\neither publishes packages nobody needed to publish or leaves a consumer pinned to an older release.\r\n\r\n- A **runtime** `dependencies` bump reaches every consumer of the published package. Every package\r\n  downstream of it re-pins, re-runs its gates, bumps, and republishes, in layer order.\r\n- A **development** `devDependencies` bump reaches nobody. Re-pin it, prove the gates still green,\r\n  and commit to `main`. Do not bump the version and do not publish.\r\n- A development bump that moves the published artifact is no longer a development bump. Prove the\r\n  direction with the build, not the diff of sources: rebuild after the re-pin and compare `dist/`\r\n  against the published tarball. Compare material content only �?\" exclude sourcemaps and ignore\r\n  whitespace-only differences; a superfluous diff (formatting, blank lines, map noise) moves\r\n  nothing and obliges nothing. A material diff �?\" tokens, declarations, logic �?\" means the published\r\n  surface moved �?\" a forced `src` or `app` edit and a toolchain-changed emit both surface here �?\" so\r\n  that package bumps and publishes on its own account, and its own dependents follow the preceding\r\n  runtime rule.\r\n\r\nEvery package is `0.0.x`, where a caret pins one exact release. A dependent therefore sees a new\r\nversion only after it re-pins and republishes, so the fleet publishes in topological layer order\r\nderived from the runtime `dependencies` and `peerDependencies` edges, never from a development\r\nedge. Layers exist for a reason a flat pass cannot fix: ranges that disagree install duplicate\r\ncopies of the same package, and the compiler reads them as distinct types.\r\n\r\nRead the order from the catalog table in `.claude/agents/orkestrel.md`, which `scaffold catalog`\r\nregenerates from the registry. Its `Layer` column is the publish round. Regenerate it before\r\nsequencing a cascade rather than trusting the copy in the tree, and never write a second order down\r\nsomewhere else.\r\n\r\nThe tooling packages sit outside that order because nothing depends on them at runtime. `scaffold`\r\nis a development dependency of every package, including packages it depends on itself, so a runtime\r\nlayering would report a cycle that does not exist. Each package builds against the already-published\r\n`scaffold`, never against an unpublished one, and a `scaffold` release therefore publishes on its own\r\nand propagates as files rather than as a cascade. A package the fleet consumes as a development\r\ndependency takes the same shape when its consumers' gates read its unpublished tip;\r\n`.agents/skills/orkestrel-publish/references/wave.md` A\u0015 Rule on the bump states that trigger.\r\n\r\n`scaffold` also carries a second published surface beside `dist/src`: `package.json` ships\r\n`dist/host`, the vendored file set every target receives through `repair`.\r\n\r\n- Bump and publish `scaffold` when any vendored byte changes, or when the set of vendored paths\r\n  changes. That surface moved on its own account, and `dist/src` need not move with it.\r\n- Re-pin `@orkestrel/scaffold` in each target after a vendored-only release, run `repair` there, and\r\n  prove that target's gates still green. `repair` restores `tests/setupPolicy.ts` and\r\n  `tests/policy.test.ts`, so a vendored-only release can turn a green target red. A target bumps\r\n  only when its own published surface moved.\r\n- Keep a target's own Claude permissions in `.claude/settings.local.json`, never in the vendored\r\n  `.claude/settings.json`. `repair` restores the vendored copy, so a `defaultMode` or an `allow`\r\n  entry added there is reverted without warning and the operator loses grants they set\r\n  deliberately. Change the vendored file only here, in the host inventory.\r\n- Never edit a vendored file inside a target. `repair` restores it, so the edit is reverted and\r\n  reports as drift in `scaffold audit`. In this repository those same files are the published\r\n  `dist/host` surface, so editing one forces a bump, a publish, and a re-propagation across every\r\n  target. Scope a fleet-wide refactor to the files each target owns, and record the vendored\r\n  exclusion in the brief rather than letting each unit rediscover it.\r\n\r\n## Acceptance laws\r\n\r\n- No writer's and no external engine's self-assessment is authoritative.\r\n- Never spend Opus 5 or Sol on absorption, distillation, scouting, or mechanical edits. Lifecycle verbs have fixed meanings.\r\n- **Boolean behavior.** A binary behavioral switch is a boolean, not a two-literal union.\r\n- **Real domain states only.** Literal unions represent irreducible modes, phases, discriminants, or external values - not decorative labels for facts already represented.\r\n- **Absence is `undefined`.** Never invent sentinels such as `'none'`, `'unset'`, `'unknown'`, `''`, or `-1`. The Writing rules in\r\n`AGENTS.md` govern its prose and are not restated here.\r\n\r\n## Authority and workflow\r\n\r\n- Read the matching spec/guide before code, form the intended design, then compare implementation. Existing code is a verification target, not ground truth.\r\n- `AGENTS.md` and its linked rules are the sole convention source. Do not create competing instruction copies in guides.\r\n- `guides/README.md` is the map: maintain both a concept index and a directory index. The concept index runs `spec �+\" source �+\" tests �+\" showcase` minus every column whose subject this workspace lacks, so an app-only workspace that publishes no library and builds no showcase still owes a full index over the columns it has.\r\n- Where the repository keeps one, `ROADMAP.md` is the sequenced plan of record. Each chunk reaches green before the next.\r\n- A showcase is executable proof of public API. A missing demonstration is a missing feature, detectable by parity.\r\n- An integration surface's guide documents the validated hookup for each supported client: the exact commands run, the authentication and approval model that client needs, and the honest limit wherever a client cannot reach part of the surface.\r\n\r\n## Parity\r\n\r\n- Every backticked API in a guide resolves to a real public export.\r\n- Every public export is documented.\r\n- TypeScript, SCSS, Markdown, tests, and showcase remain aligned.\r\n- A parity failure identifies drift; never suppress or weaken the test.\r\n- A guide `Summary` cell equals its export's doc-block description paragraph, both read in the form\r\n  the `findDrift` function compares (a `{@link}` tag written as its target's code token, whitespace\r\n  collapsed, a code span's boundary whitespace trimmed); a titled `@example` equals the guide fence\r\n  under the heading of that title; and the README pitch equals the guide's tagline.\r\n  `tests/guides.test.ts` asserts each through the `findDrift` function and the `tagline` method\r\n  that `@orkestrel/guide` exports. Invoke the public `GuideCommand` class there with the package's\r\n  inventory policy and direct host ports, and register the package assertions in the anonymous\r\n  callback passed to its `execute` method. The command owns argument validation, explicit\r\n  rewriting, and remaining-drift reporting. Run `npm run test:guides` for read-only parity\r\n  assertions. Use\r\n  `npm run test:guides -- --to guide` or `npm run test:guides -- --to source` only when choosing\r\n  an explicit rewrite direction. Before adopting the generated command, implement its direct\r\n  entry and rewrite directions in the package-owned `tests/guides.test.ts`. Scaffold must not\r\n  synthesize or overwrite that authored proof. Update each fleet package's authored file before\r\n  its release. Never weaken the gate.\r\n- The TSDoc voice rule governs a doc block, and a `Summary` cell carries that block's description\r\n  paragraph, so the same voice governs the cell. A guide tagline and a README pitch are noun\r\n  phrases, and each is the blockquote under its file's H1.\r\n- A vendored dependency guide is a mirror. Its relative links address the upstream tree and resolve to nothing here, so they are outside local-link parity. Refresh a mirror rather than rewriting it: a rewritten copy is a translation, and no comparison against the fetched bytes can check it.\r\n- Falsify a prose claim the way you falsify a code claim. The parity test proves a name exists, never that a sentence about behavior is true, so run the example and read what it returns. A `// false` beside a call that returns `true` is a defect of the same kind as a wrong return value, and it reaches every consumer who installs the package. That proof has a home: `tests/guides.test.ts` executes the flagship fences, per `.claude/rules/tests.md`. An ordered behaviour with no gate is not a gate.\r\n  Asserting that the sentence appears is not asserting that it is true. `expect(text).toContain('a spawn fault reports null')` passes unchanged when the code starts returning something else, so it guards the documentation's presence and nothing about the behaviour. Where a prose claim about behaviour sits under no fence, add the executed assertion that would break if the claim went false, and keep the substring check only as a presence guard beside it. A row whose close condition names a behaviour does not close on a substring.\r\n- Re-read the prose last, against what actually shipped. Where a change chose to document a limit rather than close it, the sentence was often drafted for the option that lost, or written more confidently than the code earns. Code rulings survive review because a test can break them; prose rulings survive because nothing tries.\r\n\r\nFor behavioral interfaces/classes:\r\n\r\n- Document public methods under `## Methods`.\r\n- Use one method table per interface, keyed by its backticked name.\r\n- The table's methods exactly match the interface's call-signature members.\r\n- Readonly data properties remain in the interface's `## Surface` row.\r\n- Each implementing class exposes exactly its interface methods�?\"no missing or extra public behavior.\r\n\r\nParity scope:\r\n\r\n- Normally scope a guide to one module directory.\r\n- A layer concept may include the core module and backend modules implementing it.\r\n- Cross-cutting environment-root helpers are covered by behavioral tests rather than forced into one module guide.\r\n\r\n## Guide examples\r\n\r\nCode fences import through the package's published specifier:\r\n\r\n- primary/core API: `@orkestrel/<name>`;\r\n- secondary environment API: `@orkestrel/<name>/<environment>`.\r\n\r\nNever use in-repository `@src/*` aliases in public guide examples; reserve them for source/tests.\r\n\r\n## Workflow skills\r\n\r\n- Skills prescribe reusable process; they do not copy naming, placement, syntax, lifecycle, or test laws from `AGENTS.md` and rules.\r\n- Where a skill works a named subject, name its directory in the `orkestrel-<verb>-<noun>` form with the verb acting on that noun; where a skill runs a process the Orchestrator owns, name it in the `orkestrel-<verb>` form.\r\n- For a portable skill that teaches an external framework, takes the host project's own `AGENTS.md` file as its code law, and binds none of this repository's rule files, drop the `orkestrel-` prefix and name the skill for the framework a reader searches for. Add no other exception. The `enterprise-bootstrap` skill meets that test and keeps its name: it carries Bootstrap 5.3 craft, assumes no stack, and names the `.agents/orchestration.md` and `.claude/rules/quality.md` files only where they are present. Do not rename it into the namespace.\r\n- Keep `SKILL.md` concise and route conditional detail to one-level `references/`.\r\n- Name every Markdown file in a skill's `references/` from its `SKILL.md`, and delete a reference nothing names.\r\n- Frontmatter contains only `name` and a trigger-focused `description`.\r\n- Set `name` to the skill's own directory name.\r\n- Write `description` as a single-line scalar or a folded `>-` block, and no other shape. Include in it a sentence beginning `Use ` that names when to invoke the skill.\r\n- Do not put model routing or package version catalogs in a skill.\r\n- Validate every referenced resource, leave no template TODOs, and limit each skill directory to `SKILL.md`, `agents/openai.yaml`, and the `references/*.md` files its `SKILL.md` names; add no other file or directory.\r\n- Write `agents/openai.yaml` as one root `interface:` mapping over exactly `display_name`, `short_description`, and `default_prompt`, in that order, each on its own two-space-indented line.\r\n- Research the external schema only when a consumer needs a key outside `display_name`, `short_description`, and `default_prompt`, and add no key before then.\r\n- Give every one of those keys a non-empty single-quoted scalar, and write an apostrophe inside it as `''`.\r\n- Name the skill's own `$<directory>` token in `default_prompt`.\r\n- Keep provider bridges minimal: they load one canonical workflow and add no competing instructions.\r\n- Give a provider bridge its canonical skill's `name` and `description` verbatim, name the `.agents/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` path it loads, and give it no references of its own.\r\n- Give every canonical skill exactly one provider bridge directory of the same name, and give every bridge directory a canonical twin.\r\n- Review a bridge body's remaining instructions yourself: the policy sweep proves `name` and `description` parity, the named canonical path, and the absence of bridge-owned references, and nothing else about the body.\r\n# Developer-facing writing rules\r\n\r\nThese rules govern prose a developer reads, including chat replies, reports, guides, README files,\r\nand commit messages.\r\n`AGENTS.md` A\u0015 Writing governs prose everywhere, and this file does not restate it; this file adds\r\nonly what a developer audience decides. An instruction file follows `AGENTS.md` A\u0015 Instruction files\r\nfirst, and these rules wherever a rule here names no different form for it.\r\n\r\n## Voice and actor\r\n\r\n- Write `must` for a requirement, `can` for an option or an ability, and `might` for a possibility.\r\n  A recommendation takes the imperative, the same form `AGENTS.md` A\u0015 Writing fixes for instructions.\r\n  Never write `should`, and never soften a recommendation into `We recommend`.\r\n- Address the developer as `you`. Name the software component that acts, and make it the subject of\r\n  the sentence. Reserve `user` for someone using software the developer builds. Never write `we`,\r\n  `our`, or `let's` about agent work.\r\n- Give software no human faculties. A component reports, returns, detects, or refuses; it never\r\n  knows, thinks, wants, or sees.\r\n- Use the passive voice only where naming the actor adds blame and nothing else, such as a defect\r\n  count. Name the actor wherever the reader needs it.\r\n- Use negative contractions (`don't`, `isn't`, `can't`) in a reply or a guide. Do not use them in an\r\n  instruction file.\r\n\r\n## Sentence and paragraph order\r\n\r\n- Put the condition, the goal, or the location before the instruction, so a reader the instruction\r\n  does not apply to stops at the first clause. Put the result or the reason after it. This governs a\r\n  sentence, not a document: a report still opens with its finding.\r\n- Put the key point in the first sentence of every paragraph and every list item, not only in the\r\n  opening of the reply or the section.\r\n- Keep the helper words `that`, `then`, `of`, `a`, and `the`. Do not drop one for brevity.\r\n- Name the noun after `this`, `these`, or `it` wherever the reader could attach the pronoun to\r\n  another referent.\r\n- State what the reader can do. Do not write a double negative.\r\n\r\n## Claims and time\r\n\r\n- Claim only what the reader can check. Never write `ensure`, `guarantee`, a superlative, or an\r\n  effort adjective as a claim about behavior, and cite the run behind every number. `ensure`\r\n  addressed to an executor as a directive is outside this ban.\r\n- Write the present tense for what exists. Do not write `currently`, `now`, `new`, `latest`, or\r\n  `soon`; where time matters, give the version or the date.\r\n- Recommend one path, the shortest one you proved. Rule on every option you list, and drop an option\r\n  equivalent to the one you recommend.\r\n\r\n## Code tokens, references, and links\r\n\r\n- Put a code token in backticks and follow it with a noun: the `parse` method, the `vite.config.ts`\r\n  file, the `--check` flag. Never inflect, pluralize, or possessivize a code token, and never use one\r\n  as an English verb.\r\n- Point to other material with `preceding`, `following`, `earlier`, or `later`, never with `above` or\r\n  `below`. Use `earlier` and `later` for a version range too.\r\n- Write link text as the destination's title or a descriptive phrase, introduced by `see`. Never\r\n  write `here`, `this document`, or a bare URL in prose.\r\n- Paraphrase third-party content and link its source; never paste it into prose you author. A\r\n  vendored mirror is fetched bytes rather than authored prose, and `.claude/rules/documentation.md`\r\n  governs it.\r\n\r\n## Structure\r\n\r\n- Keep a required fact in the main flow. A note or a notice carries only what the reader can skip,\r\n  never a prerequisite, a step, or a warning the task depends on.\r\n- In a reply or a guide, introduce every list, table, and code fence with a complete sentence naming\r\n  what follows; a rule file's list sits bare under its heading. Number a list only where order or\r\n  rank matters. Use a table only for rows with comparable fields.\r\n- Write a heading in sentence case, verb first for a task and a noun phrase for a concept. Identity\r\n  numbering �?\" a claim, an audit verdict, a plan unit �?\" is data rather than a heading style, and\r\n  stays.\r\n\r\n## Examples, numbers, and abbreviations\r\n\r\n- Build an example from fictional data with descriptive names and no personally identifiable\r\n  information. Write a placeholder in `UPPER_SNAKE_CASE` and explain it on first use. Never write\r\n  `foo`, `bar`, or `baz`.\r\n- Expand an abbreviation the reader may not know on first use, with the short form in parentheses.\r\n  Skip the expansion for one this audience reads daily, such as `API`, `CLI`, `JSON`, `URL`, or a\r\n  file format.\r\n- Write a numeral for a technical quantity, a version, or a measurement. Write a date as\r\n  `YYYY-MM-DD` in evidence, commit messages, and reports. See `AGENTS.md` A\u0015 Writing for what\r\n  separates a value from a count.\r\n- Use the serial comma. Mark omitted code with a comment in the sample's language, never with `...`.\r\n\r\n## Substitutions\r\n\r\nReplace each term in this table with its replacement. A literal code identifier is data, and so is\r\na sample string inside a code fence or a test fixture: quote each as itself, exempt from every row.\r\n\r\n| Term                     | Replacement                               |\r\n| ------------------------ | ----------------------------------------- |\r\n| `should`                 | `must`, `can`, `might`, or the imperative |\r\n| `simply`, `easy`, `just` | Delete                                    |\r\n| `currently`, `now`       | Delete, or give the date                  |\r\n| `new`, `latest`          | Delete, or give the version               |\r\n| `utilize`, `leverage`    | `use`                                     |\r\n| `via`                    | `through`, `by using`                     |\r\n| `in order to`            | `to`                                      |\r\n| `e.g.`, `i.e.`           | `for example`, `that is`                  |\r\n| `etc.`                   | Bound the list, or recast the sentence    |\r\n| `performant`, `robust`   | The measured property                     |\r\n| `allows you to`          | `lets you`                                |\r\n| `and/or`                 | `and`, `or`, or `both`                    |\r\n| `since` (causal)         | `because`                                 |\r\n| `once` (temporal)        | `after`                                   |\r\n| `please`                 | Delete                                    |\r\n| `sanity check`           | `quick check`                             |\r\n| `dummy`                  | `placeholder`                             |\r\n| `blacklist`, `whitelist` | `denylist`, `allowlist`                   |\r\n| `master`, `slave`        | `primary`, `replica`                      |\r\n\r\n- `policy/no-banned-term` reads every comment and the prose sweep in `tests/setupPolicy.ts` reads\r\n  every authored Markdown file, and each matches the rows this table bans unconditionally. The rule\r\n  and the sweep leave `now`, `new`, `latest`, `once`, `since`, and `master` unmatched because those\r\n  rows carry a permitted sense, so rule a hit in one of those rows yourself.\r\n- Sweep case-insensitively and across inflections when checking prose against the preceding table. A\r\n  pattern for `easy` reaches neither `Easy` nor `easier`, and a temporal `once` most often appears\r\n  as a sentence-initial `Once`.\r\n- Rule every hit by the sense its row bans, not by the match. `once` counts as often as it means\r\n  `after`, and `new` names a value as often as it dates one. Record a hit in a permitted sense as\r\n  permitted rather than dropping it.\r\n- Name the pattern and the paths behind every sweep result, including a clean one. A result naming\r\n  neither reports on the population its pattern admitted rather than on the population it was drawn\r\n  from.\r\n- Write singular `they` for a person of unstated gender.\r\n- This table carries no row for `execute`, `abort`, `kill`, `terminate`, or `run`:\r\n  `.claude/rules/names.md` A\u0015 Fixed lifecycle vocabulary owns those words, and no row here overrides\r\n  it.\r\n\r\n## Not adopted\r\n\r\n- Do not add conversational personality, warmth, or memorability to a reply; the prose stays plain.\r\n- Do not import a license to break these rules where a departure reads better; an exception exists\r\n  only where a rule states it.\r\n- `We recommend` as a recommendation form �?\" refused in A\u0015 Voice and actor.\r\n- Do not apply a source guide's sample indentation or line-length chrome; the repository formatter\r\n  and `.claude/rules/typescript.md` decide code presentation.\r\n- Do not write an unspaced em dash; this project spaces it.\r\n- Do not cap a sentence at a word count; `AGENTS.md` A\u0015 Writing owns sentence length.\r\n- Do not treat an external word list as vocabulary law; the preceding substitution table is the\r\n  adopted set.\r\n---\r\npaths:\r\n  - 'tests/**/*'\r\n  - 'vite.config.ts'\r\n  - 'configs/**/*.ts'\r\n  - 'package.json'\r\n---\r\n\r\n# Testing rules\r\n\r\n## Test contract\r\n\r\n- Mirror module/application structure:\r\n  `tests/{src,app}/[environment]/[domain]/[module].test.ts`.\r\n- The mirrored population is `src` and `app` alone. `configs/` is a source directory and is\r\n  deliberately not a mirrored root: its leaves produce the workspace's configuration rather than ship\r\n  in it, and they are proved from `tests/config.test.ts` beside the configuration they produce. Do\r\n  not add `tests/configs/`.\r\n- Resolve a mirrored module through `.ts`, `.tsx`, `.mts`, `.cts`, `.vue`, `.scss`, or `.css`.\r\n- Resolve a Sass or CSS partial through the module's leading underscore.\r\n- Resolve each root `tests/setup*.test.ts` proof against its sibling `tests/setup*.ts` module. A\r\n  root `tests/setup.test.ts` file can prove several setup modules when their helpers serve\r\n  several projects.\r\n- Prefer test filenames matching entrypoints: `index.test.ts` for `index.ts`, `main.test.ts` for `main.ts`.\r\n- Tests are deterministic: identical inputs produce identical results.\r\n- Keep default suites fast: timers normally use 10�?\"50 ms and tests make no network calls.\r\n- Use real implementations and small scenarios. Never use mocks, behavioral fakes, module replacement, or framework spies for project-owned or integrated behavior.\r\n- Use recorders for calls/events, temporary resources for stateful boundaries, protocol-faithful fixture servers for deterministic network peers, and the real external service when its behavior is the claim.\r\n- Prefer inert customizable data and input stubs.\r\n- Allow a scripted boundary stub only when it implements the real interface or protocol minimally, to drive the system under test. It never reimplements project-owned behavior and never stands in for the integration being claimed.\r\n- Bind a test fixture server to `127.0.0.1` on an ephemeral port (`listen(0)`), never to `::1` and never to a fixed port: a host without IPv6 fails `EAFNOSUPPORT`, and a fixed port flakes on occupancy.\r\n- Cover happy paths, error paths, empty input, boundary values, `NaN`, positive/negative zero, cycles, and Map/Set order where relevant.\r\n- Test observable behavior, not implementation details.\r\n- Assert the membership a discovered or globbed set must have, not a total that a partly empty population satisfies. A glob spanning two locations passes a size check while one of them matches nothing.\r\n- Never assert an implementation against itself. Compare the answer to a declaration, a fixture, or a second mechanism that could disagree with it. Re-deriving the answer the same way the source derives it produces a test that passes for every value the source ever returns, and it reads exactly like a real one.\r\n- Probe a host-varying property at runtime, on the host the test is running on, and assert against what the probe returned. Filesystem case folding, path separators, permission bits, and rename semantics differ per host, so a fixture built on one host describes that host and silently measures something else on the next.\r\n- Assert a runtime-chosen result as the property it must have, not as the number one run produced. Compression, timing, and buffer sizing are the runtime's choice, so pin the relationship the test depends on �?\" that the encoded form is larger, that the second call is faster �?\" and let the assertion fail when the input drifts out of the range where that relationship holds.\r\n- Measure an elapsed interval with `performance.now()`, never `Date.now()`. `Date.now()` returns whole milliseconds, so an interval built from two of its readings truncates at both ends and can under-report by a millisecond �?\" enough to fail a boundary assertion against a timer that behaved correctly. `performance.now()` is monotonic and sub-millisecond, and it does not move when the wall clock does.\r\n- Give a conditional skip the mechanism that makes it inapplicable, cited, not the platform name alone. A test skipped on a platform is a test nobody re-examines; a test skipped because a named API rejects a named case is one anybody can re-check.\r\n- A regression test records the exact command and its failing count before the fix, and the same command's passing count after.\r\n- The revert that proves a repair reddens exactly the test that names the defect. Keep the import and collection graph valid while reverting, and confirm the named test was collected. A revert that reddens anything beyond that test broke the harness, and its count is not evidence.\r\n- Use `it.todo()` only for explicitly out-of-scope roadmap work, never to complete the current request. Every `.skip` or conditional skip has a narrow verifiable applicability reason.\r\n- Do not create test files solely for `constants.ts`, barrels, error definitions, or `types.ts`.\r\n- Run the narrowest relevant Vitest project during development; do not run the entire suite casually.\r\n\r\n## Cross-cutting proofs\r\n\r\nA proof that covers the workspace instead of one module has a fixed location, so no package invents\r\nits own:\r\n\r\n| Path                         | Proves                                                                                                                                                                                       |\r\n| ---------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\r\n| `tests/policy.test.ts`       | The path- and text-shaped policy laws: mirrors, suppressions, the rule map, filenames, manifest scripts, skills, and bridges                                                                 |\r\n| `tests/config.test.ts`       | Root configuration resolves its aliases, projects, and outputs, and the `configs/` leaves behind them                                                                                        |\r\n| `tests/guides.test.ts`       | Every documented API exists, every public API is documented, every compared summary, example, and pitch equals its source, and every executable fence returns what the guide says it returns |\r\n| `tests/conformance.test.ts`  | Where this package drifts from the official tooling it tracks                                                                                                                                |\r\n| `tests/distribution.test.ts` | The packed package installs and resolves through its public exports                                                                                                                          |\r\n| `tests/integration.test.ts`  | The package's features work together end to end across environments                                                                                                                          |\r\n| `tests/setup*.test.ts`       | Reusable behavior exported from sibling `tests/setup*.ts` modules works as the workspace's suites require                                                                                    |\r\n| `tests/service/**/*.test.ts` | The live external services this package drives, driven for real                                                                                                                              |\r\n\r\n- Put each root `tests/setup*.test.ts` proof in the `setup` project. Keep its assertions on\r\n  exported test-infrastructure behavior: do not duplicate production behavior there, and do not\r\n  move setup-helper assertions into another cross-cutting proof.\r\n- `.claude/rules/workspace.md` names the Vitest project each location belongs to.\r\n- The `guides` project runs in Node with the browser disabled. Its subject is what the guide\r\n  claims: that every documented name resolves, and that every fence asserting a value returns\r\n  that value. A proof that renders a component and compares it against a definition is a\r\n  composition and belongs in an `integration.test.ts` scoped to its directory.\r\n- Transcribe each flagship fence and assert the values its comments claim. Name resolution is\r\n  not a behavioural proof, so a fence documenting a value the code contradicts passes every\r\n  parity assertion. Change a fence, change the transcription beside it.\r\n- `integration.test.ts` is a reserved filename at any level. It names a scope rather than a module,\r\n  so the mirror rule does not reach it; its scope is the directory it sits in.\r\n- An integration test is an end-to-end test: it composes the package's own features and drives them\r\n  together, through the public API, with no part of the system under test replaced.\r\n- Scope it by where the composition happens. `tests/integration.test.ts` drives features **across**\r\n  environments �?\" core through server, core through browser, one environment's output consumed by\r\n  another. A nested `tests/src/<environment>/integration.test.ts` drives features **within** that one\r\n  environment.\r\n- Do not put a packaging, install, or distribution check in an integration test. What the tarball\r\n  contains is a different question from whether the features compose.\r\n- A test the mirror rule flags is a misplaced test until its placement is checked. Move it to the\r\n  location its scope names. Widen the rule only when that check shows the test already sits at the\r\n  location its scope names and the rule's population omits a module this ruleset mandates, and then\r\n  widen the population rather than admitting the individual case.\r\n- A nested `tests/{src,app}/<environment>/**/integration.test.ts` runs in that environment's project,\r\n  whose existing glob collects it exactly once. Give it a separate exact-path project entry only when\r\n  the proof needs different setup or a different runtime, and exclude that exact path from the\r\n  environment project when you do.\r\n\r\n## Probes\r\n\r\nA probe is a throwaway instrument that settles one question. It is not a test and never ships.\r\n\r\nRoute the question before writing a probe. When you can state the edit you believe is correct and the\r\nedit that must break with the stage it breaks at, the question is a claim for the `prove` tool the\r\n`probe` MCP server registers, and `.claude/rules/quality.md` A\u0015 Instruments owns that rule. When the\r\nquestion carries no stated belief to falsify yet, when the subject lies outside the TypeScript a\r\nworkspace project judges, or when the proof needs what that tool's stages do not model �?\" a process\r\ntree, a listening socket, an installed package, a built entry driven as a child, or a live external\r\nservice �?\" write a probe.\r\n\r\nThe kinds split by which tool has to see the probe:\r\n\r\n- A **type probe** is read by `tsc`, whose scoped project includes only its own environment, so it\r\n  lives in the source tree beside what it measures. Delete it before the unit returns; a leaked one\r\n  fails the `policy` plugin's placement rules, because a probe filename is not a centralized kind\r\n  file.\r\n- A **runtime probe** is collected by a Vitest project, so it lives in `tmp/probe/` and runs through\r\n  the `probe` project. `tmp/` is ignored by git, so no probe enters a commit by accident, and every\r\n  test script names its project, so no gate runs the `probe` project.\r\n- A **bench** is read by Vitest's benchmark mode, so it lives inside a test file as a block behind\r\n  the `if (import.meta.env.MODE === 'benchmark')` guard. Only the `test:bench` script collects the\r\n  block, test mode fails an unguarded `bench()` call loudly, and no gate runs a bench. Call `bench`\r\n  directly inside the guard. A `describe` inside it trips `vitest/no-conditional-tests`, and a suite\r\n  the guard leaves unregistered fails test mode with `No test found in suite`.\r\n\r\nRun a probe before relying on an unverified belief about behaviour: what a function returns, what a\r\nconfiguration resolves to, whether a path is reached at all. Prefer a probe to an argument whenever\r\nthe probe is cheap.\r\n\r\nWhen the question is whether the difference between methods is a magnitude or negligible, write a\r\nguarded bench block beside the probe test and run the `test:bench` script. Declare the threshold\r\nbefore the run, read the ratio between the methods against each side's reported uncertainty, and\r\nrecord nothing below a magnitude. A settled magnitude that underwrites an implementation choice\r\npromotes with its test into the mirrored suite and keeps its guarded block there while that choice\r\nstands. A deterministic relationship promotes as an ordinary assertion, so delete the block. Never\r\ncommit baseline output.\r\n\r\nThese rules bind every probe:\r\n\r\n- **Prove the instrument can fail before trusting that it passed.** Pair it with a control drawn\r\n  from outside the population it covers.\r\n- **Promote or delete.** A probe that settles a claim becomes a test in the mirrored location that\r\n  proves the real source. Delete every other probe. A probe left in the suite reports on the harness\r\n  while reading as a test.\r\n- **Never commit a probe.** The ignore rule stops an accident, not a deliberate forced add, and a\r\n  type probe sits in tracked source with no ignore rule at all.\r\n\r\n## Live-service tests\r\n\r\nLive external services/models are the deliberate exception to fast hermetic defaults:\r\n\r\n- Put them in the `service` project, under `tests/service/`, with `tests/setupService.ts` for setup\r\n  and a longer timeout. That module's presence is what registers the project, so a live proof with\r\n  no readiness setup is a project nothing configures.\r\n- `.claude/rules/workspace.md` fixes which gate runs the `service` project, and that gate is not the\r\n  same one in a publishing and a `private: true` workspace.\r\n- Warm and verify service readiness in `tests/setupService.ts`.\r\n- Hard-require readiness: throw loudly; never silently skip.\r\n- Verify service-dependent logic through that service's project, not unrelated module tests or scattered conditional skips.\r\n- Tune each request to the smallest input/context/output that proves one behavior without becoming brittle or expensive.\r\n- Prefer semantic bounded assertions over exact generated prose. Increase context/workload only when the scenario requires it.\r\n\r\n## Expensive proofs\r\n\r\nA test that spawns a process, packs, installs, or drives a real build is a proof, not a unit test.\r\n\r\n- Give it its own Vitest project with its own setup and timeout.\r\n- Keep it out of the default run where the workspace has a gate that can hold it.\r\n  `.claude/rules/workspace.md` fixes which gate each isolated project runs from, and that placement\r\n  differs between a publishing and a `private: true` workspace.\r\n- The fixed expensive-proof projects are `distribution` and `service`.\r\n- A `distribution` proof reads `import.meta.env.MODE` and fails, rather than skips, on an unreachable\r\n  registry under `--mode release`. The publish gate invokes it that way, so a proof that skips there\r\n  passes the gate without ever proving the artifact installs. An ordinary local run may still skip.\r\n- Slow and hermetic is reason enough to isolate a proof; it need not touch an external service.\r\n- Where such a proof stays in a shared project, size its budget from a full contended run rather than from an isolated one. A budget that clears the isolated cost by a thin margin turns contention into a red gate reporting a timeout, which carries no diagnostic about the code and costs a full investigation to dismiss.\r\n\r\n## Shared test infrastructure\r\n\r\nTest helpers are shared infrastructure, not local test-file clutter.\r\n\r\n`@orkestrel/test` owns the helpers every workspace repeats: the call recorder, the real delay, the JSON and async collectors, and the owned scratch directory. Import them from `@orkestrel/test`, and its Node-only helpers from `@orkestrel/test/server`. Write a helper of your own only where the package exports none for the job. The following shapes are the contract a workspace codes against, not source to copy.\r\n\r\n- For the vendored test set (`tests/setupPolicy.ts`, `tests/policy.test.ts`, and\r\n  `tests/config.test.ts`), keep shared helpers within that set instead of importing them from\r\n  `@orkestrel/test`; follow the vendored-file import law in `.claude/rules/workspace.md`.\r\n\r\n- Extract a fixture, recorder, event factory, async wait, renderer, scenario/data builder, protocol fixture, or DOM builder as soon as it could serve another test.\r\n- Any duplicate or near-duplicate helper is a defect; consolidate it into one general form.\r\n- Export every reusable helper, fixture type, factory, constant, and guard from setup files.\r\n- A setup file owns everything an assertion needs and nothing an assertion is: `describe`, `it`, and `expect` never appear in a `setup*.ts`.\r\n- Data tables and case matrices belong in a setup file at any size; test registration does not.\r\n- Test files import shared infrastructure rather than declaring local fixture factories.\r\n- Never reimplement a framework helper in tests or fixtures; import the real parser, signer, flattener, or other helper.\r\n- Prefer small customizable factories/stubs that seed inert data for a real scenario over repeated inline setup.\r\n- Helper names follow module-helper naming: `createFixtureServer`, `buildElement`, `appendItems`, `renderRows`, `waitForReady`, `extractDetail`.\r\n\r\nPlace helpers by environment:\r\n\r\n- `tests/setup.ts`: host-independent; no `node:*`, DOM, `window`, or Vue.\r\n- `tests/setupServer.ts`: Node-only helpers and `node:fs` loaders anchored to `WORKSPACE_ROOT`.\r\n- `tests/setupBrowser.ts`: DOM/Vue/browser helpers and setup CSS.\r\n- `tests/setupStyles.ts`: CSS/style helpers and compiled cascade.\r\n\r\n### Recorder\r\n\r\nImport `createRecorder` from `@orkestrel/test` instead of a framework spy when only calls and arguments matter. It returns:\r\n\r\n```ts\r\ninterface RecorderInterface<TArgs extends readonly unknown[]> {\r\n\treadonly calls: readonly TArgs[]\r\n\treadonly count: number\r\n\treadonly handler: (...args: TArgs) => void\r\n\tclear(): void\r\n}\r\n```\r\n\r\n### Delay\r\n\r\nImport `waitForDelay` from `@orkestrel/test`; never repeat an inline timeout promise. It waits for one host timer and defaults to `0`:\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nfunction waitForDelay(ms?: number): Promise<void>\r\n```\r\n\r\nUse it to yield, never to wait for something another process produces. A fixed delay chosen to\r\noutlast a child's startup is a race whose loss looks like a product defect: the test measures\r\ninterpreter bootstrap rather than the behaviour it names, and it fails on a loaded host and passes on\r\nan idle one. Wait until a named condition holds instead, polling with `waitForDelay` inside a budget\r\nmeasured by `performance.now()`, and fail with the condition's own description when the budget\r\nexpires.\r\n\r\n### Scratch\r\n\r\nImport `createScratch` from `@orkestrel/test/server` when a proof needs real files. It allocates a temporary directory it owns, contains every path against escape, and removes the directory on `destroy`:\r\n\r\n```ts\r\ninterface ScratchInterface {\r\n\treadonly path: string\r\n\twrite(target: string, text: string): string\r\n\tread(target: string): string | undefined\r\n\thas(target: string): boolean\r\n\tnames(target?: string): readonly string[]\r\n\tensure(target: string): string\r\n\tlink(target: string, source: string): string\r\n\tremove(target: string): void\r\n\tdestroy(): void\r\n}\r\n```\r\n\r\n### Style primitives\r\n\r\nBrowser/style setup exposes shared assertions/builders:\r\n\r\n`mount`, `render`, `build`, `readStyle`, `readToken`, `readRootToken`, `readPixels`, `parseCSSColor`, `matchesColor`, `findRule`.\r\n\r\n`findRule` proves a declaration exists in the cascade; `readStyle()` reads the resolved result.\r\n\r\n## Browser tests\r\n\r\nUse the real browser as the system under test:\r\n\r\n- Do not replace DOM events, storage, observers, viewports, layout methods, pointer, or drag APIs unless the browser genuinely lacks one.\r\n- Prefer real nodes, events, styles/layout, and observers.\r\n- Centralize event factories: `createPointerEvent`, `createDragEvent`, `typeInput`, `fireTransitionEnd`.\r\n- Centralize DOM builders: `createButtonElement`, `createDropdownElements`, `createModalElement`.\r\n- Assert DOM state, emitted events, callback records, focus, classes, attributes, and public API state.\r\n- Do not assert private state, internal timers, or framework scheduler internals.\r\n- Prefer real short timers and observable wakeups. Never replace the host clock.\r\n\r\n## Runner configuration\r\n\r\nKeep Vitest/provider configuration minimal:\r\n\r\n- Prefer defaults until a measured problem proves them insufficient.\r\n- Centralize provider setup in one helper/shared block.\r\n- Add browser/provider/teardown/timeout/parallelism/cache/launch settings only for a current verified need.\r\n- Avoid long browser flag lists and persistent contexts unless a test requires them.\r\n- For slow teardown, inspect test cleanup, open handles, file parallelism, and context churn before adding launch flags.\r\n- Remove exploratory settings after fixing the cause.\r\n- Config comments explain the current reason, not the history of failed experiments.\r\n\r\n## Untestable usually means missing seam\r\n\r\nBefore accepting that a behavior cannot be tested, look for the seam that would make it testable.\r\n\r\n- Treat a collaborator reached through a hard-coded global �?\" a stream, a clock source, a spawn, a fetch �?\" as a missing injection point, not an untestable truth.\r\n- An injected collaborator with a real minimal implementation is a sanctioned boundary stub, not a mock of project-owned behavior.\r\n- Add the seam rather than shipping the gap whenever the design would welcome it anyway, and whenever a sibling collaborator is already injected.\r\n- Record a genuinely irreducible gap where a reader meets it: what is unproven, why it cannot be driven, and what would change that. A silent untested guard reads exactly like a tested one.\r\n\r\nCoverage rules:\r\n\r\n- Read a coverage report as a discovery input, never as evidence of proof. It answers one cheap mechanical question �?\" which code no test executed �?\" and that reliably finds forgotten branches and rules nothing calls.\r\n- Never cite coverage as the adequacy audit's result. It cannot tell you whether an executed line is asserted, so a fully covered file can still be entirely unproven.\r\n- Never let a percentage become the target.\r\n\r\n## Discovery and adequacy audit\r\n\r\nBefore acceptance:\r\n\r\n- prove every intended test file is discovered by the correct project;\r\n- prove every declared project is reachable from a gate. A project registered in the root\r\n  configuration with no script, or with a script no chain runs, is a proof that never executes �?\" and\r\n  because it never executes it never fails, so the suite reports green while carrying it. Read the\r\n  chain, not the exit code: run each project directly once and compare that list against what `test`\r\n  actually invokes. The gate that would report this gap is the gate that is missing.\r\n- prove a declared project's include resolves to a real file. An empty project is not a passing\r\n  project: Vitest exits non-zero on \"no test files found\", so a project aimed at a path that was\r\n  never created stays invisible until something finally runs it.\r\n- inspect actual test counts and environments;\r\n- audit `.todo`, `.skip`, conditional skips, retries, and inflated timeouts;\r\n- confirm each assertion would fail for the defect it claims to catch, and that it fails rather than passes when its population is empty;\r\n- confirm helpers do not reimplement production behavior;\r\n- confirm cleanup runs after setup or assertion failure;\r\n- confirm current-scope requirements have real tests rather than placeholders.\r\n---\r\npaths:\r\n  - '**/*.{ts,tsx,mts,cts,vue}'\r\n---\r\n\r\n# TypeScript rules\r\n\r\nThe non-negotiables and design laws in `AGENTS.md` apply without exception and are not restated here. This file adds only what TypeScript itself decides.\r\n\r\n## Syntax and imports\r\n\r\n- Tabs for indentation.\r\n- No semicolons unless ASI requires one.\r\n- Single quotes.\r\n- Named exports only, except framework-required defaults.\r\n- ESM imports use explicit `.js` extensions for local TypeScript modules.\r\n- Place `import type` declarations before value imports.\r\n- Do not place blank lines between consecutive imports of the same kind.\r\n- Narrow an accepted `unknown` with a total guard rather than a conditional access.\r\n- A parameter property is a constructor parameter carrying any accessibility or `readonly` modifier.\r\n  The ban reaches every form, `readonly` alone included; declare the `#` field and assign it in the\r\n  constructor body.\r\n\r\n## Types\r\n\r\n- Put every reusable or public interface/type alias in the nearest authoritative `*/types.ts`.\r\n- Public collection properties and return types use `readonly T[]`, `ReadonlyMap<K, V>`, or `ReadonlySet<T>`.\r\n- Optional state is `T | undefined`; an optional lookup failure returns `undefined`.\r\n- `as const` annotates a literal with its own type and never overrides the checker, so the assertion\r\n  ban does not reach it. Use it to derive a literal union from a value and to fix a tuple's arity and\r\n  element types. Do not write it on a value whose contract is already declared; annotate the\r\n  declaration instead. A class field that holds one literal keeps `as const`\r\n  (`readonly code = 'ABORT' as const`): the vendored lint gate's `prefer-as-const` rule refuses the\r\n  annotated form, and a field whose type is a union of literals is annotated as the preceding\r\n  sentence states.\r\n\r\n## Immutability\r\n\r\n- Never mutate caller-owned inputs.\r\n- Use copy-on-write for internal state.\r\n- Return copies or readonly views; never leak a mutable internal reference.\r\n- Compute derived facts instead of persisting duplicate state.\r\n\r\n## Errors and outcomes\r\n\r\n| Condition                            | Required strategy                           |\r\n| ------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------- |\r\n| Programmer error or invalid argument | Throw an `AppError`                         |\r\n| I/O/network/external operation       | Return `Result<T, E>` or throw consistently |\r\n| Optional missing lookup              | Return `undefined`                          |\r\n| Invalid input inside a guard         | Return `false`; never throw                 |\r\n\r\nUse the existing outcome contract from a declared dependency or the owning environment; do not redeclare it at each call site. When no such contract exists and adding a dependency is not authorized, define this once in the owning `types.ts`:\r\n\r\n```ts\r\ninterface Success<T> {\r\n\treadonly success: true\r\n\treadonly value: T\r\n}\r\n\r\ninterface Failure<E> {\r\n\treadonly success: false\r\n\treadonly error: E\r\n}\r\n\r\ntype Result<T, E = Error> = Success<T> | Failure<E>\r\n```\r\n\r\n- When `@orkestrel/contract` is declared, inspect and use its exact installed `Result`, construction/narrowing helpers, and `attempt` behavior instead of duplicating them.\r\n- Otherwise construct and narrow through the owning environment's centralized helpers.\r\n- Error classes expose a machine-readable `code` and optional `context`.\r\n- Every public error class ships with a guard such as `isAppError` for safe `catch` narrowing.\r\n\r\n## Comments and API documentation\r\n\r\n- Comments explain why, never restate what self-explanatory code does. A word ending in `s` that the rule's stop set does not name\r\n  passes whether or not it is a verb �?\" a plural noun such as `Files` included �?\" so read the sentence\r\n  in review as well.\r\n- Describe a boolean parameter as \"If `true`, �?�; if `false`, �?�\", and a boolean return as\r\n  \"True if �?�; false otherwise\".\r\n- Write a default as \"Default: �?�\" and a thrown error as \"Thrown when �?�\".\r\n- State a prerequisite and the failure behavior wherever the symbol has either.\r\n- `@deprecated` names the replacement first, then the reason.\r\n- Document an options object as one `@param`; describe its short fields under `@remarks`.\r\n- Private methods and overload-specific notes use single-line `//` comments, not public TSDoc.\r\n- Do not document speculative future product behavior unless requested.\r\n- Never use `@internal` to hide a method; make it `#` private.\r\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

They prove nothing about the claim the work makes. This skill is for\r\nthe moment when everything passes and the work is still not known to be right.\r\n\r\n## Load authority\r\n\r\n1. `AGENTS.md`.\r\n2. `.claude/rules/quality.md`, especially **Falsification** �?\" the laws governing auditor behaviour.\r\n   This skill prescribes the round; that section prescribes the conduct inside it. When the previous round's claims all held, nothing has\r\nbeen added or repaired since, and the only motive is that an auditor could still imagine an attack,\r\nthere is no subject �?\" closing is the correct action and the next subject is the deliverable.\r\n\r\nWhen the same class has recurred along one seam to the budget in `.claude/rules/quality.md`\r\nA\u0015 Rounds and verdicts, the next move is that law's strategy switch �?\" the breadth sweep of the\r\nstream, the ruling, or the dropped moving-target claim �?\" rather than another successor round.\r\n\r\n## Write the brief\r\n\r\nThe brief is the instrument. A weak brief produces a confirming review no matter which auditor reads\r\nit. Follow `references/brief.md`. What this skill adds beyond the conduct law:\r\n\r\n- Every re-run is a **successor**, not a restatement. It carries what the previous round closed so\r\n  nothing is re-reported, and it adds claims that attack **the previous round's own rulings**.\r\n- The brief names what the round **decides**. An auditor that does not know the stakes calibrates to\r\n  politeness.\r\n- Unknowns are named as unknowns, with how the auditor reports back on them.\r\n\r\nThe claim form itself is the Falsification law's �?\" read it there. The **verdict shape** below is this\r\nskill's, because `.agents/orchestration.md` assigns it here; everything else about auditor conduct is the law's.\r\n\r\n## Evidence, by subject type\r\n\r\nThe brief supplies the evidence its subject actually has. Requiring a diff for a subject that has no\r\ndiff is a rule the brief cannot satisfy.\r\n\r\n| subject                                 | required evidence                                                                     |\r\n| --------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\r\n| a code change                           | the actual diff and the actual status output; omitting either is a dispatch deviation |\r\n| a rendered or externally driven surface | the capture portfolio as primary, source as corroboration                             |\r\n| a policy, design, or process proposal   | the proposal, the canon it must satisfy, and the record of what motivated it          |\r\n\r\n**A subject can occupy more than one row; supply every row it occupies.** A ruling whose fixes\r\nalready landed as edits is both a proposal and a code change, and withholding the diff on the grounds\r\nthat the subject is \"a proposal\" leaves the auditor unable to check whether a fix changed anything\r\nnobody claimed.\r\n\r\n## Run the round\r\n\r\n- **Verify every authority the brief references exists in the tree the auditor is rooted in.** A\r\n  brief that points at a rule file, section, or guide the executor cannot find delivers nothing while\r\n  looking like authority �?\" and it fails silently, because an auditor does not report a heading it\r\n  never saw. Check before dispatch; propagate the missing file rather than restating its contents in\r\n  the brief. This is the reason restatement felt necessary, and it is the wrong cure. It reproduces each stated vector itself and defaults to\r\n  refuted when uncertain.\r\n- Refute on any of these grounds, and name which: the vector does not reproduce; the behaviour is\r\n  correct and documented; it is unreachable through the public API or a documented seam; it asks\r\n  for new capability rather than naming a defect; it restates a finding an earlier round\r\n  repaired; or its diagnosis is wrong �?\" then CONFIRM with the correction.\r\n- Only a survivor earns a fix unit. An unrefuted finding is a hypothesis. The scope grounds,\r\n  unreachable and new capability, are what keep a round from drifting into a redesign.\r\n- **Give every auditor the means to run its attacks.** A lens that can only read returns derivations,\r\n  and a derivation reads exactly like a verdict �?\" it will confirm a claim that one probe would break.\r\n- **Tell each auditor exactly where a probe may live, and verify that place works before you say it.**\r\n  A test runner resolves only what its own configuration includes: a probe written outside every\r\n  configured project is not discovered at all, and the run reports no test files rather than a result.\r\n  Where the repository provides a probe project, use it and name that project in the brief;\r\n  `.claude/rules/tests.md` governs what may live there. Where it does not, the reliable form is a file\r\n  inside the canonical mirrored suite, run by explicit path. Either way the probe is deleted before the\r\n  auditor returns, and promoted into a permanent test when it proves something worth keeping. Give each\r\n  concurrent auditor a distinct filename that already satisfies the repository's test naming\r\n  convention; never invent a prefix to dodge collisions, and never let two auditors claim one path. A\r\n  probe left in the mirrored suite is discovered and fails a run nobody else caused.\r\n- **Run auditors concurrently only when their writes cannot collide.** A lens that can execute\r\n  still writes probes; give each a distinct path and forbid whole-project runs, or serialize the\r\n  round. **This binds\r\n  the orchestrator too:** a tree-wide gate run while a round is live sees the auditors' in-flight probes\r\n  and reports a failure nobody caused. Wait for the round, or scope the command to paths no auditor\r\n  owns. Never delete another executor's working file to make your own command pass.\r\n- **Tell a lane when its own engine wrote the half it is auditing, and tell it to attack that half\r\n  harder.** A fix round reviewed by the engine that wrote it is the case the round exists to avoid,\r\n  and where the pass cannot avoid it, naming it is what recovers the round. A clean pass on its own\r\n  engine's work is the least valuable result a lane can return.\r\n- Supply the evidence the subject type requires, per the table above.\r\n- Auditors edit no source and spawn nothing. Read-only describes the SUBJECT, never the lane's\r\n  tools.\r\n- **Read the lane's allowlist before writing its brief.** A lane with no write tool cannot create\r\n  a probe; a lane with no exec tool cannot run one or read `git`. Naming either stops the unit on\r\n  arrival over a detail the allowlist already settled.\r\n- Where the lane cannot execute, run the probe yourself, record its control and its output, and\r\n  supply that record as the lane's evidence. The Orchestrator produces, the lane rules. Never\r\n  widen a lane's tools to fit a brief.\r\n- Blind reports are **immutable**. Nothing an auditor returns is edited, merged, or revised �?\" by\r\n  anyone, including the auditor �?\" once it has been returned.\r\n\r\n## Verdict shape\r\n\r\nEvery auditor in every round returns exactly this, and nothing else. One shape makes rounds\r\ncomparable; a round that invents its own cannot be read against the last one.\r\n\r\n1. **Numbered verdicts**, one per claim, in the brief's order. Exactly one of these values:\r\n\r\n   | value           | meaning                                                                                | evidence                                                                                                                                                      |\r\n   | --------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\r\n   | `CONFIRMED`     | attacked and it held                                                                   | as the Falsification law requires                                                                                                                             |\r\n   | `BROKEN`        | falsified                                                                              | as the Falsification law requires �?\" note it says _input, **state, or interleaving**_, so a concurrency claim is falsified by an interleaving, not by an input |\r\n   | `UNRESOLVED`    | cannot be decided from the evidence available                                          | what would settle it; a claim whose only evidence is the writer's own report takes this value                                                                 |\r\n   | `NOT-EVIDENCED` | a claim about a rendered or externally driven surface the supplied capture cannot show | which capture is missing                                                                                                                                      |\r\n\r\n   `CONFIRMED` and `BROKEN` defer; only `UNRESOLVED` and `NOT-EVIDENCED` are this skill's, because\r\n   the law does not name them. `BROKEN` and `UNRESOLVED` are **separate**: a claim nobody could\r\n   decide has not been falsified, and it cannot supply the fields falsification requires.\r\n   `NOT-EVIDENCED` is the auditing lanes' own token; it is kept, not re-invented.\r\n\r\n2. **Findings fitting no claim**, if any, each substantiated to the same standard as `BROKEN`.\r\n\r\n3. **Attacked and held**: the claims this round attacked and could not break, each with the attack\r\n   that failed, and the adjacent behaviour that looks like the defect and is correct. This is where\r\n   the next round reads what has already been tried, so a claim listed here without its attack is\r\n   worth nothing to it. A claim's own `CONFIRMED` line already carries the evidence that convinced\r\n   the auditor, so list here only the attacks no verdict line carries and the adjacent behaviour.\r\n\r\n4. **One terminal line**, and only one:\r\n\r\n   ```text\r\n   VERDICT: PASS\r\n   VERDICT: FAIL <claim numbers>; outside the claims: <finding ids>\r\n   ```\r\n\r\n   The claim numbers are every claim that is not `CONFIRMED`. Write `none` in a slot the round\r\n   leaves empty, so a `FAIL` driven only by a finding outside the claims still names both.\r\n\r\n   **`PASS` requires all of this**: every claim `CONFIRMED`, nothing `UNRESOLVED`, nothing\r\n   `NOT-EVIDENCED`, and no substantiated finding outside the claims. A single substantiated finding\r\n   forces `FAIL` however the numbered claims landed �?\" otherwise a round can report a real\r\n   defect and still emit the word that authorises the release.\r\n\r\nNo process diary. No summary of what was read.\r\n\r\n## When a round comes back all-confirmed\r\n\r\nThe Falsification law governs this: the brief goes on trial, not the subject. Apply it, and if the\r\nclaims turn out to have been descriptive, the successor brief is where the sharper ones go.\r\n\r\n## Reconcile and rule\r\n\r\nFollow `references/reconcile.md`. The obligations that are not delegable:\r\n\r\n- **Reproduce every sharp finding yourself** before acting on it. An auditor's finding is a\r\n  hypothesis until the orchestrator has run it.\r\n- **Build before you pack.** `npm pack` runs no build, so it ships whatever `dist/` is on disk. An\r\n  auditor who packs an artifact to inspect it is reading the last build, not the current source, and\r\n  will report deleted exports as still shipping. Run the package's build first, and say in the brief\r\n  that the tarball was built from the commit under audit.\r\n- **Check the subject before acting on a finding, not only the reasoning.** A brief that names attack\r\n  vectors teaches the lane those vectors matter, and a lane can hand back the brief's own questions as\r\n  the subject's claims �?\" demanding coverage for a property the subject never documented. Grep the\r\n  subject for the claim the finding rests on. Where it is not there, the finding is against the brief.\r\n- **A disagreement between auditors is rarely a tie to average.** It is usually correct answers\r\n  to different questions. Find the question each one answered.\r\n- **Bound every finding**: state what is _not_ broken, and why the adjacent behaviour that looks the\r\n  same is correct. An audit that reports everything is as useless as one that reports nothing.\r\n- **Bound the fix before briefing it.** Establish what over-correcting would break, and include that\r\n  in the fix brief as a constraint.\r\n- Drop, on the record, any finding neither auditor can substantiate.\r\n\r\n## Accept, or run it again\r\n\r\nThe threshold is **a `PASS` terminal line on a brief whose claims cover what the subject owns** �?\"\r\nevery numbered claim `CONFIRMED` on evidence, nothing `UNRESOLVED`, nothing `NOT-EVIDENCED`, no\r\nsubstantiated finding beside them. Never green gates, which prove only that a suite ran.\r\n\r\nA round that finds something is a success, not a delay. A substantiated finding against something no claim names is real and forces\r\n`FAIL`; an unsubstantiated one is a claim for the successor brief. The escalation law in\r\n`.claude/rules/quality.md` governs a subject that keeps producing findings at the same seam �?\" after\r\nenough of them the ruling owed is on the design, not on the next defect.\r\n\r\nWhen a fix round follows, its auditor must be an engine that did not write it, and the next round's\r\nbrief is the successor of this one.\r\nwarning: unable to access 'C:\\Users\\mikes/.config/git/ignore': Permission denied\nwarning: unable to access 'C:\\Users\\mikes/.config/git/ignore': Permission denied\n2178171f2e4cb4530175ec3e4571b3952b9674e9\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

A brief written in the orchestrator's\r\n  repository and staged into the subject's checkout so a `-C` invocation can reach it is a second\r\n  file, and staging can rewrite a path or drop a clause. The executor rules on what it opens, so a\r\n  staged copy whose facts are false stops a unit that was correctly briefed. Verify the staged path\r\n  and its load-bearing facts before launching, and stage into a scratch directory the subject tree\r\n  ignores rather than into the checkout root.\r\n- Send a decision taken mid-campaign to every unit already in flight whose brief it invalidates. An\r\n  executor cannot see a change made after it was dispatched, so it writes the state its brief\r\n  described and the defect surfaces as its own.\r\n- Retention is uniform for every unit, whatever engine ran it, including an Orchestrator-owned\r\n  integration, fix, probe, or capture unit: copy the brief, the returned report or distillate, the\r\n  audit verdict, the exact executed script or instrument, and the acceptance evidence into\r\n  `.orkestrel/<package>/` as the unit is dispatched and as it returns, then sweep only the `tmp/`\r\n  launch copies. A capture claim's instrument is acceptance evidence; the frames may be swept once\r\n  the record transcribes them, because the committed instrument re-produces the film. The **Bench\r\n  laws** rule \"Ephemeral streams, durable records\" owns journals and points here for everything\r\n  durable.\r\n- Name a retained log with the `<unit>.log.txt` pattern, never with a bare `.log` suffix, which the\r\n  root `.gitignore` file ignores.\r\n- Promote anything that must outlive the campaign into a durable artifact before the sweep �?\" a\r\n  commit message, a guide, a rule, a retrospective. What is only in a swept file did not survive,\r\n  and a debrief that must quote the record verbatim has nothing to quote.\r\n- Land a process rule stated as binding mid-campaign in the owning rule or contract file in the\r\n  same commit that states it. A campaign artifact is evidence, never a rule's home.\r\n\r\n### Where campaign artifacts live\r\n\r\n- Put every campaign artifact in the **orchestrator's** repository under `.orkestrel/<package>/`,\r\n  named for the package the campaign is about.\r\n- Give a campaign spanning several packages one shared `.orkestrel/campaign/` folder instead, so the\r\n  wave's plan, routing ledger, and verdicts sit together rather than split across the packages they\r\n  rule on.\r\n- Never put them in the package they are about. A published package's tree is its product.\r\n- Where the orchestrator's repository is itself a subject package, keep `.orkestrel/` as the\r\n  artifact home and stage every landing chain by path, never with `git add -A`. Run that\r\n  checkout's own CLI through the built `node dist/bin/main.js` entry, never through the `npx`\r\n  launcher, and record its audit reading beside the landing instead of gating the landing on it.\r\n  Commit the records before the landing's commit step, never during it.\r\n- Claim nothing outside `.orkestrel/` unless Orkestrel scaffold mandates it. Everything Orkestrel\r\n  owns in a consumer's tree lives beneath that folder, so a convention can be settled there without\r\n  colliding with a convention that is not Orkestrel's.\r\n- Keep the campaign narrative and every ruling in the durable artifact that owns it �?\" the guide for\r\n  product truth, a rule or role file for process truth, the commit message for the decision itself.\r\n  Use `ROADMAP.md` only where the repository already keeps one.\r\n- Prefer a mechanism that recomputes a fact over a document that records it. A document recording\r\n  live state is stale from the moment it is written, and the next campaign reads it as current.\r\n  Where the fact can be derived, derive it: the fleet's publish order lives in the catalog table\r\n  `scaffold catalog` regenerates, not in a written order anyone has to remember to update.\r\n- Prune the campaign folder in a commit at acceptance. Git history is the archive; the working tree\r\n  is the workspace. `.agents/skills/orkestrel-debrief/references/retention.md` owns the procedure �?\"\r\n  the checks that close the prune, the artifact locations it sweeps, and the promotion record\r\n  the commit message carries. Run it before deleting anything.\r\n\r\n### Required sections\r\n\r\n- **Role and engine.** The named role and its explicit engine.\r\n- **Objective.** One concrete outcome.\r\n- **Context.** The evidence slice, paths, decisions, `AGENTS.md`, applicable rules, the skill name\r\n  and its required references (or an explicit none), and the guide or spec. Include the host\r\n  environment facts the unit will hit �?\" the shell, path, and network constraints its commands run\r\n  under �?\" because an executor that rediscovers them pays for the discovery in round trips. State\r\n  every standing condition the same way: a file expected to be dirty, a command known to fail, a\r\n  shim the shell blocks. A condition the brief leaves unnamed comes back as a deviation report\r\n  about something you already knew.\r\n- **Unknowns.** What the Orchestrator does not yet know that the unit needs, named as unknown, with\r\n  how the unit reports back on it. A brief that cannot be fully specified says so instead of\r\n  shipping a guess the executor would have to invent an answer around.\r\n- **Scope.** Owned files, shared and off-limits files, allowed tools, permission limits.\r\n- **Execution.** State that the executor performs the assignment directly and spawns nothing. Put\r\n  it in every brief; an executor deep in a task does not re-read this contract. Write the sentence\r\n  for the reader the transport actually delivers it to, because \"directly\" names a different action\r\n  on each side of a bridge. To a native subagent or a bench engine reading the brief inside its own\r\n  CLI, it means do the work yourself. To a bridge driver, whose entire assignment is the launch, it\r\n  means carry the brief across unaltered and return the journal �?\" the engine behind the CLI is not a\r\n  subagent the driver is spawning, and a driver told to work directly answers from its own engine\r\n  instead. No process diary.\r\n- **Deviation contract.** The required stop-and-report behaviour for writers, scoped. A conflict\r\n  with the primary objective stops the unit. An ancillary conflict �?\" where a paragraph sits, which\r\n  heading a section takes �?\" is the executor's to decide, record, and carry on from. An unscoped\r\n  contract stops a unit over a detail it was equipped to settle.\r\n- **Acceptance criteria.** Independently checkable completion conditions.\r\n- **Review evidence.** What the subject type requires, per the table in `orkestrel-falsify`. For a\r\n  code change that is the actual diff and the actual status output; omitting either is a dispatch\r\n  deviation. For any claim about a rendered or externally driven surface, the capture portfolio is\r\n  the review input and source is corroboration. A subject may occupy more than one row �?\" a ruling\r\n  whose fixes already landed as edits is both a proposal and a code change �?\" and it gets the\r\n  evidence of every row it occupies.\r\n\r\n### Check the brief before you send it\r\n\r\nFill `.agents/templates/brief.md`, which carries the named scope rows and the worked reason behind\r\neach check. A bridge\r\n  driver and the bench engine inside that driver's CLI need opposite instructions.\r\n- Paste the command and its output behind every factual claim �?\" paths, counts, registrations, file\r\n  existence.
